## CAP-U46-01 Project definition, lifecycle, stages, templates and roles

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** A project is the container for tasks, milestones, status updates, finances and collaborators. It has a manager, a customer, a schedule, a stage (when stages are enabled), a privacy level, a company and an analytic account. Projects can be created from templates, archived, duplicated and shared. Behaviour is read from the project model, stage model, template wizards and settings.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Objects: project, project stage, project tag, project role, project update, project collaborator, task type (task stage), analytic account link, settings. A project owns tasks, milestones, updates and collaborators; it links to analytic accounts per analytic plan column. Seeded task stages and project stages come from data files.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Create project -> analytic account created when needed -> stage default from first stage -> tasks created inside. Duplicate/template -> copies tasks, stages and roles through copy helpers. Archive cascades to tasks. Post-install hook enables sharing rules and syncs analytic plan columns.

State diagram:

    Project (no stage) -> Project in stage  [stages group enabled and stage set]
    Project active -> Project archived  [archive action]
    Project template -> Project  [create from template]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Create a project, add tasks and update statuses; see project model claims. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Archive and unlink behaviour is listed in the claims; deleting a project with analytic lines is subject to analytic rules. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company rule restricts projects to allowed companies; analytic account company follows project company. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Analytic account creation, mail followers, stage change notifications and rating schedule. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Stages, milestones, recurring tasks, task dependencies and roles are optional groups; settings toggle them. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Constraints on dates, company consistency and stage deletion are listed in the claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | Groups user and administrator plus portal; project record rules by privacy and membership. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | No project-specific schedule beyond the daily rating job that belongs to the rating capability. |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | User errors raised on invalid operations are listed in the claims. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Analytic accounts carry cost lines; no direct journal entry is written by the project itself. |

Claims in this capability: 115 (VDR-U46-C001 .. VDR-U46-C1182).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: project groups user, administrator, milestone, stages, recurring tasks, task dependencies present; 1 project and 4 task records exist in total (contents not read); task stage rows are present; 19 project menu entries.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Whether stage fold sequence seeding in the restored database matches the data files (15 stage rows counted; sequence listing looked duplicated) - RT
- Template copy behaviour at runtime - RT

## CAP-U46-02 Project access control, visibility, collaborators and portal sharing

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** Controls who can see and edit projects and tasks: privacy levels, membership, followers, collaborators from outside, and portal sharing. Internal staff are grouped as user and administrator; portal users receive a limited shared view.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Groups, access list rows (56 in DB), record rules (31 in DB, 30 active), collaborator model, project sharing wizard, portal controller, task visibility rule and private task rule.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Request -> access list per model and group -> record rules by privacy and membership -> portal route checks token or collaborator row -> allowed fields and actions bounded by sharing view.

State diagram:

    Project private -> Project shared with collaborators  [collaborator added]
    Collaborator read -> Collaborator edit  [access level changed]
    Portal visitor -> Portal visitor with link  [valid token]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Members and followers see and edit their projects; collaborators see their shared projects. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Removing collaborator or membership removes access at next read. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company rules restrict everything to allowed companies. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Following and notification behaviour follow access changes. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Privacy level per project; one portal task sharing rule is inactive in the database. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Domains of rules and constraints on collaborator uniqueness are listed in claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | Declared groups, access rows and rules as listed in the claims and DB reconciliation. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | No schedule. |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Access errors redirect portal visitors. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Security-relevant: sudo use in controllers and rule overlap are flagged RT. |

Claims in this capability: 118 (VDR-U46-C088 .. VDR-U46-C1184).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: 56 access rows and 31 record rules (30 active) owned by project; the portal task sharing rule is present but inactive; the task visibility and private task rules are active.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Effective access for a given user needs runtime evaluation of overlapping rules - RT
- Portal sharing edge cases - RT

## CAP-U46-03 Task lifecycle: stages, assignment, dependencies, recurrence, closing

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** A task is a unit of work with title, project, stage, assignees, deadline, priority, tags, dependencies, subtasks, recurrence and a closing state. Personal stages give each user a private pipeline. Computed values include effort, progress, days and activity counts.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Task model, task type, personal stage, task tag, recurrence model, dependency relations, mail template links, burndown tracking values, analytic lines for timesheets are in other modules.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Create -> default stage and assignee rules -> write handlers (stage change sets closing date, notifies, triggers rating and recurrence) -> close -> recurrence creates next occurrence. Duplicate copies fields and subtasks selectively.

State diagram:

    Task open -> Task closed  [stage with closing flag or state change]
    Task closed -> Task open  [reopen]
    Task cancelled -> Task open  [reopen]
    Task waiting on dependency -> Task unblocked  [dependency closed]
    Task recurring -> New occurrence  [close with recurrence]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Task flows through stages to a closing stage; see claims. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Reopening, cancellation and duplicate behaviours are listed in the claims. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company follows project; allowed companies rule applies. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Notification, rating request, recurrence, analytic and burndown tracking side effects. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Stages group, dependency group, recurring group, subtasks flag per project. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Constraints and onchange validation listed in claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | User and administrator group differences, private tasks, portal rules. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | Recurrence creation happens on close, not on a timer; no cron here. |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Raised errors listed in claims. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Tracking values in the audit trail feed the burndown report. |

Claims in this capability: 228 (VDR-U46-C128 .. VDR-U46-C1186).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: 4 task records exist in total (contents not read); task type rows present; no scheduled task activity beyond the rating job.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Recurrence boundary dates at runtime - RT
- Dependency cycle detection behaviour at runtime - RT

## CAP-U46-04 Milestones, project updates, burndown and task analysis reports

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** Milestones mark deliverables with a deadline and reached flag; project updates post periodic status with progress and tracked task counts; burndown and task analysis reports are read-only views built on tracking values and task rows.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Milestone model, update model, burndown report view over tracking values, task analysis report view, last-updated milestone helper, rules per model.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Reached flag change -> milestone state; update create -> status computed from project and tasks; report views built with SQL joins and grouping.

State diagram:

    Milestone open -> Milestone reached  [flag set]
    Milestone open -> Milestone late  [deadline passed while open]
    Update on track -> Update at risk or off track  [status chosen]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Create milestones, post updates and read reports. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Unreach and delete behaviours listed in claims. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company rules apply to milestone, update and report models. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Messages posted on update; reached flag drives analytics. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Milestone group optional; reports available to project groups. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Constraints listed in claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | Visibility and admin rules for milestone and update; report rules. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | No schedule. |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Not applicable beyond validation. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Report SQL has duplicate and id collision risk flagged RT. |

Claims in this capability: 49 (VDR-U46-C338 .. VDR-U46-C1188).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: milestone and update visibility rules present and active; burndown rules present.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Burndown id collision effects - RT
- Task analysis duplicates - RT

## CAP-U46-05 Customer rating

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** A rating records a satisfaction score from a partner about a record, collected via a tokenised link, a mail template, or a stage-triggered request. Aggregates feed averages on the rated record.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Rating model with token, rated object reference, partner, rated person, feedback and consumed flag; rating mixins and parent mixin for aggregates; controller for submit; project task and project hooks; digest KPI.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Request created -> link with token sent -> visitor submits value via controller -> rating consumed and aggregates recomputed -> optional stage-trigger sends new request. A daily job in the project module sends periodic requests.

State diagram:

    Rating requested -> Rating consumed  [customer submits via link]
    Rating consumed -> Rating re-submitted  [link revisited with new value]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Customer opens link and submits a score; see claims. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Re-submission and invalid token behaviour in claims. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company follows the rated record. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Aggregates, notifications and chatter message. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Per-stage template, periodic job, project rating toggle. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Token and value checks in claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | Access rows: internal users read-write-create, public none; token access through controller. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | Daily project rating job (active in DB). |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Invalid token returns a not found or error page. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Tokens are bearer secrets in links; GET link does not record. |

Claims in this capability: 70 (VDR-U46-C509 .. VDR-U46-C1190).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: 4 access rows and 1 menu owned by rating; the daily project rating job is active.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Behaviour when the mail template is missing - RT
- Digest KPI computation at runtime - RT

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Profitability item hooks, analytic account links, bridge models per module, project stock account model, sale and expense links.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Panel open -> each bridge contributes items with sequence -> amounts converted to project currency -> totals computed; bridge hooks write analytic distribution on lines.

    Cost invoiced -> Cost reversed  [credit note]

## CAP-U46-08 Purchase: requests for quotation, orders, receipts, vendor bills and extensions

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** A buyer drafts a request for quotation, sends it, confirms it as an order (with optional two-step approval), tracks receipts and vendor bills, and may reuse the flow through agreements, repair links, grid entry and electronic document import and export. Vendors can open orders on the portal, acknowledge, and update expected dates.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Purchase order, order line, bill line match, bill to order wizard, purchase report, purchase bill union, product and partner extensions, company and settings fields, analytic and tax links, portal controller; extension modules for requisition, repair, grid, EDI.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Draft -> sent -> (to approve) -> purchase -> cancel by action; confirm computes dates, adds vendor to products, locks per policy; bill creation groups by order and writes lines; bill auto-complete matches lines; portal updates dates and acknowledges.

State diagram:

    Draft -> Sent  [send action]
    Draft or Sent -> To approve  [confirm above threshold]
    Draft or Sent or To approve -> Purchase  [approve or confirm below threshold]
    Any -> Cancelled  [cancel action; no state check]
    Cancelled -> Draft  [draft action; no state check]
    Purchase -> Locked  [lock action or company policy]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Quote, confirm, receive, bill as in claims. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Cancel and set-to-draft apply no state guard; credit notes handled in bill creation. |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company rules on orders, lines, union and report; company consistency constraint. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Vendor price lines added, activities, mail, analytic, tax usage, bill and receipt links. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Lock policy, approval policy, threshold, warnings, reminders, matching, agreements, grid, EDI are optional. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Constraints and warnings in claims. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | User, administrator, accounting and portal rows listed in claims and DB. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | Daily reminder job (active in DB). |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Errors on missing fields, tax country, duplicate and merge refusal. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Vendor bill and journal entry creation; down-payment SQL precedence and approval currency conversion flagged RT. |

Claims in this capability: 375 (VDR-U46-C783 .. VDR-U46-C1196).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Restored DB: 35 access rows, 8 record rules (all active), 4 groups, daily reminder job active, sequence prefixed P with 5 digits; company set to locked, two-step approval, threshold 5000; no orders.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Approval threshold currency conversion - RT
- Down-payment matching SQL precedence - RT
- Electronic document conformance - RT

## CAP-U46-10 External call interfaces (legacy XML/JSON and bearer-key JSON interface)

- Function-ID: FUNCTION MAPPING REQUIRED

**D1 business purpose and process semantics.** Provides the standard remote model access endpoints: two deprecated legacy interfaces (XML with string or integer fault codes, and a JSON one) and the current JSON interface that calls a public model method with a bearer key.

**D2 architecture, data and object relationships.** Controllers for the legacy XML interface (two variants), the legacy JSON interface, a public version endpoint and the current JSON interface; fault-code constants and value marshalling helpers. No models, access rows or rules.

**D3 source, technical and workflow logic.** Legacy: request -> cursor closed -> service dispatch with in-service authentication -> result marshalled or fault returned by error kind. Current: POST model/method -> bearer key authenticates -> method resolved (public only) -> arguments checked against signature -> call under read-only or read-write cursor -> result serialised (record sets as ids).

State diagram:

    Legacy call -> Result  [service dispatch succeeds]
    Legacy call -> Fault  [user error, access error, denial or other exception]
    Current call -> Result  [public method, valid arguments]
    Current call -> Not found or Unprocessable  [private method, ids on model-level method, bad arguments]

| # | Dimension | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Happy path | Authenticated call returns the method result in the interface format. |
| 2 | Reversal / negative path | Failures return faults (legacy) or not found or unprocessable responses (current). |
| 3 | Multi-company / data scope | Company scope is that of the authenticated user; not read in this module. |
| 4 | Side effects and cross-module triggers | Side effects belong to the called method; the context mapping can be supplied by the caller. |
| 5 | Configuration and optionality | Module is auto-installed; legacy endpoints are flagged deprecated with scheduled removal. |
| 6 | Validation and constraints | Argument signature check and public-method rule on the current interface. |
| 7 | Roles and permissions | No groups or access rows declared; authentication by service or bearer key. |
| 8 | Scheduled / automated behaviour | No schedule. |
| 9 | Exceptions and failure behaviour | Fault codes one to four and traceback exposure on unexpected errors in legacy interface. |
| 10 | Accounting, stock, audit, security implications | Authentication surface; legacy no-session endpoints and traceback in faults are security-relevant. |

Claims in this capability: 23 (VDR-U46-C1151 .. VDR-U46-C1198).

**DB reconciliation (configuration only).** Config only: module installed; owns no access rows, rules, groups or schedules in the restored database.

**Unknown / RT list.**
- Behaviour under concurrent calls - RT
- Bearer key resolution is outside this module - UNKNOWN

## CONTRADICTIONS WITH EARLIER UNITS (CONTRA)

CONTRA-CHECK: no claim in this unit is flagged CONTRA. The prior U16, U06 and U07 claim texts were not re-diffed line by line in this session; the cross-checks below are noted as items to verify:

- project_todo access rows give all internal users full create/read/write/delete on tasks, task stages and tags with a rule limited to private tasks; compare with U16 project access claims (not diffed).
- The dashboard filter on state 'done' for purchase orders refers to a state that does not exist (OBSERVATION; compare U06/U07).

## RT LIST

Claims flagged RT or UNKNOWN: 19.

- VDR-U46-C271 (project/models/project_task.py:1788): Cover image auto-selection compares the attachment type to the bare word image rather than a prefix match; an exact match would rarely hold, so the behaviour mu
- VDR-U46-C319 (project/models/project_task_type.py:236): Lines 236: the periodic job iterates all tasks of the stage's projects, not only tasks currently in the stage; whether tasks in other stages are filtered later 
- VDR-U46-C358 (project/models/project_update.py:182): The query at lines 157-184 ends with a limit of one, so only a single changed milestone is reported although the label implies several; runtime verification req
- VDR-U46-C383 (project/report/project_task_burndown_chart_report.py:181): The synthetic row id combines project, stage and date numerically; collisions across projects or stages with large ids need runtime verification.
- VDR-U46-C396 (project/report/project_report.py:102): Grouping by the depends-on column in the view may yield multiple rows per blocking task when it blocks several tasks while the row id is the task id; runtime ch
- VDR-U46-C617 (project_hr_expense/models/project_project.py:77): Only expenses in the posted, in-payment or paid state are counted; the expense state list includes an in-payment value that must be confirmed against the expens
- VDR-U46-C654 (project_purchase/models/project_project.py:144): The state filter on purchase lines uses a plain string rather than a list; only confirmed orders are intended, and the effective matching of the string operand 
- VDR-U46-C726 (project_todo/models/res_users.py:34): The systray split is computed by a hand-written query on the activity and task tables and bypasses record rules; it was not executed.
- VDR-U46-C752 (project_sms/models/project_task.py:27): The write hook triggers on every write containing the stage key even if the stage did not change, so repeated writes of the same stage could resend the message;
- VDR-U46-C771 (project_mail_plugin/controllers/project_client.py:47): The project-creation endpoint performs no explicit permission check of its own, relying on the standard access rules at creation.

- VDR-U46-C826 (purchase_product_matrix/models/purchase.py:47): The dirty-cell payload is parsed from client-supplied JSON without validating that the cell attribute values belong to the chosen template; the product template
- VDR-U46-C861 (purchase_edi_ubl_bis3/models/purchase_edi_xml_ubl_bis3.py:11): The purchase builder reuses a large set of invoice-side helpers in a shared library outside this unit; the exact output was not generated, so conformance to the
- VDR-U46-C965 (purchase/models/purchase_order.py:222): A separate compute for the company-currency total divides by the rate but the field is actually computed by the main amount compute, so this method appears unus
- VDR-U46-C967 (purchase/models/purchase_order.py:1257): The threshold conversion starts from the currency of the current environment company but passes the order company to the converter; for orders of another compan
- VDR-U46-C1083 (purchase/models/purchase_bill_line_match.py:104): Down payment lines with a billed quantity are also listed, and because the OR is not inside the confirmed-state condition (lines 102-104) they are listed regard
- VDR-U46-C1098 (purchase/controllers/portal.py:105): The history key test uses a string, not a one-item tuple, so it is a substring test; it still yields the RFQ history for sent and the order history otherwise.
- VDR-U46-C1103 (purchase/controllers/portal.py:155): Opening the page with the acknowledge flag sets the acknowledged marker through a read-style request; anyone holding the access link can do it.

## NOT READ

- Views, menus, QWeb templates, mail templates, report templates, static JS/XML (except grep-level), tours, demo data and migration scripts of most modules.
- Odoo base web client behaviour for portal pages.
- Full diff against earlier units' claim texts.
- Not claimed complete.
